SAFETY NOTICE

   This equipment generates high voltage which       is dangerous to life.
At all times, be careful and observe every safety   regulation. Keep clear
of all live parts. Never make or change electrical  connections while the
unit is in operation.

   Do not remove any guards, shields, or screens while the unit is in opera-
tion. Keep tools, oilcans, bolts, etc., away from the unit. Such items might
fall into the generator or be drawn into the rotating parts by magnetic
attraction. Keep moisture away from the unit and keep surrounding
area dry.

Figure 8.—Power Unit PE-215-C—automatic                                  215-E are six-pole, four-wire, two-
                                   cut-off.                            phase units which operate at a driven
                                                                        speed of 1,200 revolutions per minute.
                                                                         They are directly connected to the
                                                                         engine and are rated at 50 kilowatts,
                                                                         62.5 kilovolt-amperes, 60 cycles, 80
                                                                       percent power factor. The frame is of
                                                                       dripproof construction to protect
                                                                       current-carrying parts from dripping
                                                                         moisture and falling particles and
                                                                         objects. The rotor rides in a ball bear-
                                                                         ing at the outboard end while the
                                                                        engine end is supported by the pilot
                                                                         bearing at the flywheel end of the
                                                                         engine. The alternators are cooled by
                                                                         a current of air which is circulated
                                                                        by fans at each end of the rotor. The
                                                                         maximum temperature rise at 100
                                                                       percent continuous load is 40° C.

                                                                               Note.—The voltage rating of Power Unit
                                                                             PE-215-C is 120/208 volts while that of:
                                                                             Power Unit PE-215-E is 120/240 volts.

                                                                  (2) Exciter.—The exciters of Power Units
                                                                         PE-215-C and PE-215-E are mount-
                                                                        ed on the tops of the alternators and
                                                                         driven from the alternator shafts by
                                                                        means of dual V belts. The exciters
                                                                        are shunt wound, operate at 2,000
                                                                        revolutions per minute, and are rated
                                                                        at 1.5 kilowatts, 64 volts, and 23.4

    (1) General—Basically,   Units PE-215-C
          tem of both Power                                                                     from the fuel oil storage tank, through

                                                                      UEL SUPPLY              the primary fuel strainer, by the fuel
                                                                  UMP STRAINER                supply pump. This pump delivers fuel
                                                                                              oil, under pressure, to the air trap
      Figure 28.—Fuel supply pump and strainer.                                                or day tank. Excess oil that is de-
56                                                                                             livered to the day tank is returned

                                                                                                to the storage tank through a return
                                                                                               line. Oil from the day tank is fed to

                                                                                              the fuel oil filter by the transfer pump,
                                                                                              which is a part of the fuel injection

                                                                                               pump assembly. From the fuel filter,

                                                                                                the fuel is fed to the fuel injection

                                                                                              pumps. The injection pumps are timed
                                                                                              so that fuel is delivered to the indi-
                                                                                              vidual spray nozzles of the various
                                                                                               cylinders at the precise moment that
                                                                                              combustion is desired. The speed of
                                                                                                the engine is controlled by varying
                                                                                              the stroke of the injection pump
                                                                                               plungers and thus controlling the

                                                                                                amount of fuel fed to the injection
                                                                                               nozzles. This variation is automat-
                                                                                                ically accomplished by the governor
                                                                                               mechanism cr it may be manually
                                                                                               accomplished by adjustment of the

                                                                                                governor control.

                                                                                     f. GOVERNOR. The governors used on Power
                                                                                  Units PE-215-C and PE-215-E are fully in-
                                                                                  closed, flyweight type, and an integral part
                                                                                  of the fuel injection pump assembly. The gov-
                                                                                  ernor shaft is gear-driven from the injection

                                                                                  pump camshaft. The centrifugal force of the

                                                                                  weights is spring balanced, and as the engine
                                                                                  operates, either under load or at no load, any

instructions in paragraph 30. Protect the equip-
ment by covering it with a tarpaulin or other        ment with a tarpaulin or other suitable
suitable covering. If possible, store the com-
plete equipment within a building. The switch-       covering.
board should be boxed in a wooden box, the
interior of which has been suitably protected           b. If the equipment is to be shipped a con-
with weatherproofing material.                       siderable distance, process the equipment in

92. Shipment                                         Pack the equipment in suitable crates or boxes
                                                     in accordance with applicable Joint Army-
   a. If the equipment is to be moved a short        Navy Specifications listed in appendix I.

Section Il. DEMOLITION TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

93. Methods of Demolition                            crankcase, engine control panel, switchboard,
                                                     voltage regulator, all meters, all gages, fuel
   a. Smash. Use sledges, axes, hand axes, pick-     injection nozzles, drive pulleys, intake and
axes, hammers, crowbars, and heavy tools.            exhaust manifolds, and generator and exciter

   b. Cut. Use axes, handaxes, and machetes.         castings.
   c. Bend. Use sledges, axes, handaxes, pick-          b. Cut the fan belts, accessory drive belts,
axes, hammers, crowbars, and heavy tools.
    d. Burn. Use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame      exciter drive belts, radiator hose, fuel lines,
throwers, and incendiary grenades.                   lubricating oil lines, switchboard wiring, all
    e. Hxuplode. Use firearms, grenades, and         connecting wires and cables, and generator and
PENT.
   f. Dispose. Bury in slit trenches, fox holes,     exciter windings.
and other holes. Throw in streams. Scatter.
                                                        c. Bend the unit skid base, valve push rods,
   Note.—Use anything immediately available for de-
struction of this equipment.                         engine connecting rods, engine crankshaft,
                                                     engine camshaft, exhaust piping, and tools.
94. Destruction of Components
                                                        d. Burn all fuel oil, gasoline, lubricating oil,
   When ordered by your commander, destroy           wires, cables, generator and exciter windings,
all equipment to prevent its being used or           switchboard wiring, technical manuals, tech-
salvaged by the enemy.                               nical bulletins, supply bulletins, other docu-
                                                     ments, crates, and packing cases.
   a. Smash the cooling system radiator, air
cleaner, crankcase breather, cooling fan, fuel          e. Explode the fuel oil storage tank and all
injection pump, fuel supply pump, fuel filters,      other parts that can not otherwise be de-
lubricating oil filters, engine cylinder heads,
engine cylinder block, engine pistons, engine        stroyed.

                                                     equipment.
                                                        g. Destroy everything.
